# SPEC-0058: Main story progression

- Answers: proactive, none requested (main story order: quest table story rows, acts and heroes, walkthrough from New Game to the endings); R-0099, R-0103 (flag 239's live setter path); R-0104 (Rope Gun source and Glade branch; the physical need is settled in row 39 and Open question 15); R-0148 (Gil the Fence, Drool and Lump in pack 1139 are talkers, not fights); R-0151 (ordered goal list for an automated player, Act 1); proactive (verify pass: pack 1397 plate scenes and lift order, start-room join); R-0197 (goal 374: the burrow gates); R-0215 (the timeout ending's tick numbers and what follows movie 80); R-0218 (goal 1: flag 706 is set again in the opening, by the Farm activation thread); R-0219 (goals 28, 300, 301: flag 249 stays clear); R-0220 (goals 70, 72, 88: hall volumes, floors and stand points); R-0222 (goals 113 and 114: Painter Howell is present straight after the council scene); R-0223 (goal 556: how stage 4 of the Petpet shrine is won); R-0225 (the shop copies beside 1427); proactive (open question sweep, final fight entry); proactive (open question sweep, story routes); proactive (open question sweep, route geometry); proactive (open question sweep, Fyora palace route); proactive (open question sweep, hero traversal); proactive (open question sweep, Act 3 and Altador routes); proactive (open question sweep, ground tool recheck); proactive (spec consistency pass); proactive (story dry run); proactive (flag and walk survey); proactive (open question sweep, round 12); R-0255 (the pack 842 reward; answered from the code and disc data, not run in the original); R-0282 (goal 132: Seradar does not fire trigger 8; answered from the code)
- Status: Draft
- Disc build: SCUS-97367 v1.00 (same fingerprint as SPEC-0001)
- Confidence: per section. Quest row kind, title and objective text fields and the area-name lookup: High. Act-start calls, act flags and swap modes: High. Stage-table checkpoint meaning: Medium-High. Order of steps inside an act: Medium (quest-row order plus flag dependencies). Boss names per pack: Medium; boss instances 1, 5 and 12: High for the script match, Medium-High for the entity order. Routes between areas: Medium-Low where they rest on cell exits or the area-pair table. Return-world calls (1174 to 1300, 1327 to 886, 1122 to 1300, 1428 to 1411, 1411 to 1432, 1432 to 1300): High. Pack 1432 has no entry other than the 1411 return world: High. Script setters of the main-story flags: High for the setter sites, Medium for what each puzzle is. Pack reach (SPEC-0006): 174 of 191 packs are enterable from New Game. Per-step confidence is in the walkthrough table.

## Summary

The main story is a chain of progress flags (SPEC-0049) that level scripts (SPEC-0030 and the level-script specs SPEC-0032, SPEC-0037, SPEC-0039, SPEC-0041 to SPEC-0045) set and test across four acts. The game's own quest table names every step: its first 158 rows are the main story and its last 80 rows are side quests. A step is listed in the journal while its start flag is on and its done flag is off (SPEC-0049, Quest steps and the journal). The player controls Tor alone in Act 1, Roberta alone in Act 2, and both heroes with free switching from the start of Act 3 (SPEC-0053). Each act begins with an act-start call. The story ends in the Darkest Faerie fight (SPEC-0054), either with the finale movie, which leads through the epilogue in pack 1300 to the credits, or with the timeout ending. With this spec the clean side can lay out the story order, check that every story step has a flag setter, a route into its area and a way out, and build an automated story test that walks the steps in order.

## Where it lives on the disc

- Quest rows: archive resource 533, data table 1 (238 rows of 36 bytes; SPEC-0024 table 1 and SPEC-0049 Quest rows). Rows 0 to 157 are the main story, rows 158 to 237 side quests. High.
- Area names: archive resource 533, data table 5 (77 rows of 8 bytes; SPEC-0024 table 5). The quest row's scene id indexes this table. High.
- Stage table (the start-point checkpoints): archive resource 533, table 11 (SPEC-0049, Stage table rows).
- Act starts, flag writes and tests, movies, boss-fight instances, item gifts and area changes: the level scripts of the world packs named below (SPEC-0030 calls TriggerActStart, SetGameState, QueryGameState, PlayFMV, BossFightTweakHero, GiveItemToHero, SetHeroSwapMode, SwitchHero, SetGameOver and the transition calls). Packs are archive resource numbers (SPEC-0006).
- Area-pair table: boot program data at virtual address 0x31EEE0, 25 entries of two 32-bit values (SPEC-0006, area-pair test). The values are world-pack archive resource numbers: 23 pairs, then two zero entries. Read it at run time; it is not copied here. Medium-High for the values being pack numbers.

## Layout

### Quest row fields used by the story (table 1)

This adds meanings to the SPEC-0049 layout; the other fields are as given there.

| Offset | Size | Type | Meaning | Confidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| +0x00 | 4 | u32 | Row kind: 0 = main story, 1 = side quest. Rows 0 to 157 hold 0, rows 158 to 237 hold 1. | High |
| +0x04 | 4 | s32 | Start flag (SPEC-0049). | High |
| +0x08 | 4 | s32 | Done flag (SPEC-0049). | High |
| +0x0C | 4 | u32 | Title text: offset from the start of the table block (SPEC-0024, Pointers and string relocation) to a NUL-terminated single-byte string. | High |
| +0x10 | 4 | u32 | Objective text: same rule as +0x0C. All 238 resolve. | High |
| +0x20 | 4 | s32 | Scene id: row index into table 5 (area names), or -1 for no single area. | High for the lookup |

### Area names (table 5)

| Offset | Size | Type | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| +0x00 | 4 | u32 | Area name: offset from the start of the table block to a NUL-terminated string. All 77 resolve. |
| +0x04 | 4 | u32 | Run-time count (SPEC-0024); zero on disc. |

The area names are game text; they are read from the disc at run time and are not copied here.

### Act structure by quest rows

| Act | Main quest rows | Hero control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 0 to 69 | Tor only |
| 2 | 70 to 89 | Roberta only |
| 3 | 90 to 115 and 121 to 141 | Both |
| 4 | 116 to 120 and 142 to 157 | Both |

Rows 116 to 120 sit between Act 3 rows in table order but belong to Act 4 (the rescue of Queen Fyora and the Orb). Medium-High.

## Behavior

### Acts and heroes

| Act | Starts in pack | Act-start stage (SPEC-0053 3.7) | Swap mode after | Progress flag stored | Intro movie |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 909 | 1 | 0 (locked to Tor) | none | 88 (with movie 1) |
| 2 | 1174 | 2 | 1 (locked to Roberta) | 220 | 89 |
| 3 | 1327 | 3 | 2 (free; Tor selected) | 248 | 90 |
| 4 | 1428 | 4 | 2 (free) | 332 | 91 |

Confidence: High for the calls and stored flags. The heroes join for good at the Act 3 start, at the end of Roberta's escape from Faerieland. In pack 909 the act-start call is made after movies 88 and 1 are queued and before they are shown: queued movies play at that pass's movie step, in queue order, so the act start is already applied when movie 88 is shown (High; step 2).

Local hero locks inside Acts 3 and 4 (Medium): pack 1244 sets mode 1 or 0 and activates one hero; pack 1246 sets mode 2 after the Werelupe King; packs 1371 and 1394 switch heroes and set mode 3 during their puzzles; pack 1432 switches heroes during the final fight (SPEC-0054). In packs 1122, 1217 and 1428, a dead Roberta sends the game to game over when Tor is active, or locks control to Roberta when she is active, unless a Potion of Revival (item 50) is held (SPEC-0055, SPEC-0026).

### Stage table as checkpoints

The start-point chooser labels (SPEC-0035) match the stage ids of the stage table one to one. Each stage is a designer checkpoint of the flag state:

| Stage id | Checkpoint |
| --- | --- |
| 0 | Act 1 done |
| 1 | Act 2 done |
| 2 | Act 3 done |
| 3 | Act 3 middle |
| 4 | Squire accepted |
| 5 | Squire chores done |
| 6 | Act 3 up to entering the dungeons |
| 7 | Act 4 legends done |

Confidence: Medium-High (from the chooser labels and the flags each stage sets). The chooser always loads the default packs (SPEC-0006), so a stage start sets flags but does not move the hero. The stage contents are a useful cross-check for the walkthrough below.

"OQ n" points to an open question that leaves a link in the chain unexplained.

Pack 909 grants the Rope Gun (item 36) if absent after the Free Tor's Farm flag is set; the Act 3 story order places this farm visit before the Glade segment. The grant is part of the Farm-return scene and happens only when the Farm loads from the exit plane of pack 1217 (the message of type 2, value 50, for world 68), flag 6 is on and item 36 is absent: the hero is placed at locator 28 (-1822.1, 50.0, 1190.4), walks to locator 27 (-1830.4, 50.0, 1189.4), waits 1.2 s, and after lines 175 to 177, one tick, a camera cut and a 1.0 s wait, item 36 is given with its pick-up notice; line 178 and the rest of the scene follow. A Farm entry without that message gives nothing (so the goal list leaves the Act 3 burrow by its farm plane, goal 354, rather than walking to the farm later), and a hero who already holds the item gets no scene (High for the chain, Medium-High for the order of the scene). In pack 956, lacking item 36 changes the message, but the same callback activates aim targets 0–2 whether the item is held or not; the item check does not set a story-completion flag. Resolved (computed from the ground data, not played): a rope anchor is physically required for the climb out of the lower glade. The walkable lower ground (basin floors at y -12.2 to -8.8 and the long slope down from the approach, steps of at most 2.0) reaches the lift-bottom dais (y 4.38) only by ledge hangs, the smallest climb being two hangs of 6.6 each at the south-west corner (from (-775, -818) at y -8.8 to the terrace at y -2.2, then to the dais; the margin of each against the hang window is about 0.1 to 0.4, so Medium), and from the dais to the shaft-top plateau (y 67.7) the rise is 63.35 with one middle platform at y 27.1 (22.75 above the dais, 40.6 below the plateau). No two ground samples within 30 units across the gap differ by less than 35.8 (39.7 under 14 units). A standing jump peaks 3.20, the step-up is 2.5, and a mantle or hang reaches about 6.95 above the ground at the wall foot at most; the climb panels of the pack (at x -308 to -209 and at x -529) do not link the lower ground to the dais top, the plateau or the glade slab, and the lift is off in Act 3 (flag 64). Solution: switch 0 twice and switch 2 once, in any order (High; SPEC-0041). ### Endings

Confidence: High for the calls, the epilogue setters and the credits gate; Medium that the hero reaches the epilogue trigger volumes.

1. Finale (pack 1432, level flag 4; SPEC-0054 and SPEC-0049 Ending; the fight is entered at locator 0 in the alcove, see the final-fight paragraph below): movie 85 plays, then the game returns to pack 1300 (world 151, entry message value 80) with progress flags 94 and 365 on.
2. Epilogue: the finale's return adds blackboard message 2 with value 80 for world 151 (pack 1300). Pack 1300's activate-world callback reads that value, sets flag 366 and places the hero at landing 2 (Open question 9). Script trigger 2 (366 on, 367 off) sets 367 and plays movie 79. Script trigger 3 (367 on, 368 off) fades out, plays movie 83 and sets 368. The finale runs when the hero reaches the trigger-1 cylinder of pack 1432 inside the 15 s landed window (SPEC-0054, Trigger 1 and the finale gate). High (no play check).
3. Credits: on the next play pass after 368 is set, the in-play gate on flag 368 sets the credits-pending flag and opens the credits screen (SPEC-0026, Credits; SPEC-0035, section 6). Cross and Triangle are ignored during this roll. At its end the world is torn down, which clears every flag, and the front end returns to its boot loop (logo, attract, title). The credits can also be opened from the front-end Options page; that roll returns to Options.
4. Timeout ending: when the final fight's clock reaches 0, the screen fades out (0.5 s), movie 80 is queued and the game-over flag is set (SetGameOver(1)) on the same tick, and movie 80 is shown in that pass. The game-over page follows on the next pass; it is the same page a hero death leads to, and it freezes the world and waits for a new Cross press on page pass 122 or later. Nothing else is scripted after movie 80: no further movie, no credits and no return to another pack. Without a save or load made earlier in the session the exit goes to the card state, the logo, the attract screen and the title; with one, Continue re-applies that snapshot (SPEC-0026 and SPEC-0055). Test vector (Tor selected, no swaps, crossing the entry rectangle on tick 0): clock start on tick 74, flag 2 on tick 10,874, game over and movie 80 on tick 10,906, earliest exit about tick 11,029 (SPEC-0054, Test vectors). A hero game over is the right end state; it is not a death and changes no health.

### What the walkthrough shows about the chain

- **Rope Gun targets in Illusen's Glade (High for the target data and the gaps below, Medium for route necessity).** Pack 956 has three active aim targets at (-712.090, 79.337, -831.990), (-673.268, 53.885, -827.594) and (-720.682, 34.656, -838.226). The first lies 11.337 units above a y=68 surface; the third lies 33.344 units below that same surface. The middle target is 6.523 horizontal units from the nearest triangle in its closest walk mesh, whose height is y=8.312, 45.574 units below the target. The target points therefore do not form an obvious sequence of walkable landing points. Pack 956's item-36 branch activates the targets and changes Illusen's message, but it does not set Glade completion when item 36 is absent. The navigation meshes do not model jump arcs or ledge climbs, so the need for the rope is taken from the ground grid instead (row 39 and goal 397): the dais that carries the lift bottom is 63.35 below the shaft-top plateau, the smallest gap between the walkable lower ground and the plateau or the glade slab is 35.8 or more against a reach of 6.95 for a jump into a ledge hang, and the lift is off in Act 3; the rope is therefore the only way up. The callback still does not check that the rope was used: the story flags on the way (281 from trigger 10, 282 from trigger 11) are set by walking into the triggers. Resolved (aim from a hang; High for the state gate): the hero cannot aim the next pull while hanging from a ledge, block or grab line, or while roped; the aim scan needs the hero in state 1 or 13, and a roped hero stays in the rope state until he lets go. The chain of pulls therefore goes through standing points: pull to target 2, let go and land on the middle platform (y 27.1), then aim at target 1, and so on. The pack's single grab line lies over 230 units from the targets and is no part of this route. Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): require the hero to be on the ground before each aim. Related pack variants repeat target lists, so a target's presence alone does not establish a unique story gate.
- The final-fight pack 1432 is entered only by the return world that movie 72's call sets in pack 1411. No cell exit and no other script call leads into it. That call needs flag 338, which only movie 71's call sets, and movie 71 needs flag 337. So flag 337 gates the final fight itself, not only the two movies. On arrival the hero is placed at locator 0 in the alcove (SPEC-0039, module 1432), followed by the fight-start step (SPEC-0054). Flag 337 is set by the six-pedestal puzzle in pack 1382, reached from 1411 through 1391 and 1381 (step 46; Open question 1).
- Reach (SPEC-0006): 174 of the 191 world packs are enterable from New Game. The main-path packs 745, 752, 777, 1017 and 1394 are among them (door, script load and cell routes in steps 32, 34, 30, 35 and 45). Two of them leave by script loads: 1017 back to 991 and 1394 back to 1353. Pack 1077 is an unused copy of pack 1129, whose module it shares; step 41 runs in 1139 and 1129. It stays one of the 17 unreached packs (High). The other unreached packs are duplicates (678, 686, 970), a second zone variant (1323), cut variants (1259, 1298), a cut pack (1170), a closed pair (683 and 1003), and seven packs that nothing names (684, 720, 742, 879, 1004, 1055, 1082).

## Goal list for an automated player (Act 1)

Purpose: an ordered list of concrete goals that takes a test player from New Game through the Gnarfas fight, built from the walkthrough above, the level-script specs (SPEC-0037, SPEC-0039, SPEC-0041 to SPEC-0045), SPEC-0049 (flags), SPEC-0022 (dialogue), SPEC-0021 (items), SPEC-0048 (interactables) and SPEC-0006 (doors and entries). Goals 1 to 99 cover steps 1 to 19 and the first scene of step 20. Confidence is per row (H High, M Medium, L Low); a row marked M or L names the link that is not settled.

How to read the table.
- Kinds: TALK (stand in the character's talk range and press Square; the entity id is the entity-table id of the pack), PICK (touch a world item), SQUARE (press Square inside a script-trigger volume or at a prompt), DOOR (enter a door rectangle and confirm the Enter prompt with Square), WALK (reach a point or volume), PUSH, CLIMB, FIGHT, GAME (a minigame with its win rule) and WAIT (a scene or movie runs; control returns at its end). A row with two kinds is done in the order named.
- Positions are world coordinates (x, y, z) of the pack named. A "slab" is a trigger rectangle given as footprint centre, width along its long axis, depth, and bottom y. A "cylinder" gives base centre, radius and height; a "sphere" gives centre and radius. A door rectangle is entered anywhere along its width; its centre is given. Floor y values are approximate (plus or minus 1).
- "Done" lists the flags (SPEC-0049) or items that are true when the goal has been met. After a load, skip a goal when its Done condition already holds. "Needs" lists flags or items that must be on first; a goal whose Needs is not met cannot succeed.
- Walking legs between packs need no goal of their own: the hero walks over the ground and the next pack streams in (SPEC-0006, cell exits and world swap). The ground neighbours used by Act 1 are: 909 - 691 - 1066 - 696; 696 - 1023 (town), 696 - 1184 (Shadowglen Woods), 696 - 1230, 696 - 1056 and 696 - 893; 893 - 851; 851 - 842 and 851 - 800; 1230 - 706 - 956. Pack 1184 leads into 1217 by a script trigger. The coordinates in a WALK row are the aim point inside the destination pack. High for the neighbour pairs (they come from the pack neighbour lists); the exact crossing point inside each pair is Medium.
- Interior exits: to leave an interior, walk into its exit rectangle (centre): 951 (17.5, -0.8, 22.5); 1148 (-0.1, -2.7, 16.2); 1150 (6.5, 0.0, 8.3); 1131 (0.0, 0.0, 17.5); 1133 (0.0, 0.0, 16.1). Each leads back to the exterior it was entered from (1023 for 1131, 1133, 1148 and 1150; the farm for 951). No flag is needed except where a row says so.
- Town doors of pack 1023 (door index, interior, centre): 0 pack 1098 (65.5, 23.9, 609.5); 1 pack 1150 (-126.0, 24.4, 482.1); 2 pack 1172 (349.9, 26.5, 383.4); 3 pack 1143 (383.1, 24.3, 532.8); 4 pack 1136 (386.5, 42.0, 854.8); 5 pack 1148 (193.4, 29.9, 866.1); 6 pack 1131 (7.3, 24.0, 817.1); 7 pack 1133 (-132.9, 26.3, 739.4); 8 pack 1140 (-28.0, 24.7, 390.8); 9 pack 1139 (433.8, 33.6, 649.3); 10 pack 1129 (404.8, 25.5, 659.2). Doors 1 to 8 need flag 136 on. Door 0 needs (150 on and 11 off) or (150 off and 15 on). Door 9 needs 13 on; door 10 needs 35 on (SPEC-0045, pack 1023).
- Entity ids for the farm family (pack 909): Lucy is entity 31 (ids 31 to 36 are her six regional placements), Patricia (Mum) entity 37 (ids 37 to 42) and Hubert (Dad) entity 43 (ids 43 to 50). The stage rule of SPEC-0045 (Activation thread) decides which placement is loaded, so talk to whichever of the family is standing in the farm.

No walk, fall or ledge climb (up to 6.7) joins any of these surfaces to the training ground except through one step of 10.8 at nest 36, so the hero does not stand next to a nest. A melee swing is a cylinder of radius 1.0 and length weapon reach plus 2.0 from the hand, and a jump peaks 3.2 above take-off, so a melee hit from the training ground reaches none of the five nests (18 or more above the feet). A hit only needs to reach the nest's cylinder (a registered script hook for the breakable starts on any attack that touches it, SPEC-0036), so the practical way is a ranged attack: Roberta's shots, or a Mote power attack, aimed up at each nest from the ground below. Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): make Roberta the active hero, walk to the ground point nearest each unbroken nest (horizontal distance about 10, the order is free) and shoot at the nest's origin; if a nest is still whole after 60 s, break it by script (set its flag, 625 to 629 in order) so the quest cannot block. Gaps settled in this list.
- Entity ids of the farm family (Lucy 31 to 36, Patricia 37 to 42, Hubert 43 to 50) come from the proximity routines' entity lists; step 3 of the walkthrough did not name them.
- Item sources for step 7: the shield (item 106) lies in pack 1131; items 40, 41 and 43 lie in the village; item 42 comes from the shop talker in 1148 (the sword trade gives it with no money); item 60 comes from the smith in 1133; item 38 from Torak. Item 1 (Letter of Consent) is never tested, so it is not needed. Item 0 comes from pack 951's story thread when 147 is on.
- The ground neighbour chain for the cell routes, and the aim point in each pack (positions from the pack trigger and locator tables).
- Pack 1171's route: it is entered from 1173 (not from 1098 directly), through 1152; the lever in 1173 opens the way only after flag 162 is on (set by the 1152 villager).
- Door and trigger volumes for every goal that needs a position, taken from the packs' trigger tables and matched to the module specs where they give one (trigger indices, extents and the footprint centre rule of SPEC-0028).
- The farm pliers route is a crate push plus two ladders (SPEC-0048); Medium.
- The 1148 talkers (Kendrick, the shop talker) and the route from the lift top in 956 to the 985 entry slab (goal 96), both listed in the rows above.

Open items and defaults.
- Pliers route (goals 11 to 13): Resolved (M): the shelf, the loft floor at y 61.4, the second ladder and the roof are connected as goal 12 describes; the ladder grab from the loft is L. Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): follow goals 10 to 12 and report the goal blocked when the item is not reached.
- Resolved: the positions of entities 35, 36, 39 and 49 in 1098 (goals 51, 70, 72, 77, 88), the breakables of 1171 and 1056 (goals 64 and 71) and the talkers of 1133, 1150, 1152 and 1163 come from the entity-id table and the character regions it names. Resolved (goals 32 and 36): the pack's own character block holds three characters in 1148 (ordinals 0 to 2: Guardsman Yavik at (34.6, 0.0, -20.0), Officer Kendrick at (-0.1, 0.0, -34.9) and Tark Gutterunner at (26.0, 1.9, -27.2)); the entity table sends entity 1 to Kendrick and entity 3 to Tark, and all three stand still. H for the names and points (the block's placement records are read in the same layout as the character regions). Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): talk to the nearest talker.
- Resolved (goal 94): the platform volume runs the ride and the two spheres only call it. Resolved (goal 96): the walk to the 985 entry slab is the long climbing walk listed in the goal row (about 1,120 units, waypoints with heights); M for the line, L for a few single height jumps of up to about 2.9 along it.
- Resolved (goal 41): a world swap back into the farm re-runs its activation and plays movie 5 (H for the rule). Fallback if the movie does not play: leave 909 through house door 0 and come out again.
- Attack-state numbers in goals 54, 59 and 68 refer to the attack table of SPEC-0036.

## Goal list for an automated player (Act 2)

Purpose: the goals that carry a test player from the aftermath in pack 956 (goal 99) through the end of Act 2 and the Act 3 start, built from walkthrough steps 21 to 27, the module sections of SPEC-0043, SPEC-0044, SPEC-0045, SPEC-0037, SPEC-0039 and SPEC-0041, SPEC-0048 (blocks), SPEC-0028 (the pad puzzle of pack 1299), SPEC-0053 (hero setup) and SPEC-0021 (items). Goals 100 to 155. Conventions, kinds, position rules and confidence tags are those of the Act 1 list. Positions are in the pack named; a slab is given as footprint centre, width, depth and bottom y, a plane as centre, width and bottom y.

Heroes, items and bosses in Act 2.
- Goals 100 to 110 are played by Tor (swap mode 0). Goal 111 (the Act 2 start) makes Roberta the selected hero and locks the mode to Roberta (mode 1) until goal 155. No hero switch is possible or needed in between. At goal 155 (Act 3 start) the mode becomes free and Tor is selected (SPEC-0053, section 3.7). High.
- Inventory: at goal 111 the whole Act 1 inventory and wallet go to a stash and Roberta receives Red Juppies x3 (150), Purple Juppies x3 (149), the Diplomat Dress (127) and 1,150 Neopoints. So Tor's Light Mote (170), Fire Mote (160) and sword are unavailable until goal 155 returns the stash. Roberta has no weapon until goal 121 and her attack button does nothing before then (SPEC-0036). Heal with a Juppie from the quick panel when health is low; there is no Potion of Revival in the kit, so a death in Act 2 is game over (SPEC-0055). High.
- Motes: the only Mote Act 2 needs is the Fire Mote (item 160), 100 given by the script at goal 125 plus 1 given inside line 52 (101 in all). Equipping is optional: open the second quick panel with Down, pick the Fire Mote and confirm (SPEC-0021, Second quick panel and Mote equipping). Free Motes in pack 1300 (item 170 at (91.8, 1538.4, -78.8) and (-63.1, 1531.0, -105.3), item 162 groups, item 166, item 172) are optional pickups. Medium.
- Enemies in Act 2 (High; from the character sets each pack loads in the Act 2 flag states and the placements on disc; all are common enemies, SPEC-0038, SPEC-0064, SPEC-0065). Goals 101 and 102 (pack 1098, Tor alone, story stage 30): six Dark Knights (150 health, wandering over a square of side 90 about their home points; one stands 18 units from the arrival point), plus the idle Darkest Faerie and King Skarl at the throne. Pack 1174: the escape run above. Pack 1300 before the bed: no hostile character. Pack 1327 before the bed: only the lesson target. After the bed (flag 92 set): pack 1335 holds 13 hostile minions (set 0, replaced by 14 once flag 241 is set); pack 1344 holds nine wandering minions, loaded only when the hero first stands in it after the bed (its slot thread steps only while 1344 is the current world, so none are bound while the hero is in 1300, 1324 or 1335; SPEC-0006, SPEC-0039); pack 1324 holds none (one wild petpet); pack 1299 holds three Evil Harris (75 health) that wander over the stair mesh beside the two pads (each picks ground mesh 0 at its first tick; SPEC-0037); pack 1327 (goal 154) holds five Biting Spellbooks (35 health, flying) and no librarian staff. At most six minions of one set are bound at a time (SPEC-0006). Death with Tor or Roberta locked (swap mode 0 or 1) is game over (SPEC-0055).
- Boss fights: none in Act 2 is won by killing. Boss instance 3 in pack 1174 (goals 104 to 110) is an escape run: the Faerie Sisters (alternate loop) cannot be hurt and the Darkest Faerie (loop A) stands still at (0, -126.25, 97.2) in section 0, throwing the hero when he comes within 10 horizontal units (no damage; SPEC-0011, SPEC-0054). Entry: goal 103 (hero placed at locator 0). Exit: goal 111 (end rectangle, trigger 1). The Faerie Sisters' real fight is Act 3 (step 43). High.

Gaps settled in this list.
- Where the escape run starts and which volume starts it: 1098's trigger 6 is on the second floor; the plane and rectangle triggers of 1174 with their planes; the section scenes and locators.
- Wand-part sources: item 10 and 11 are floor placements in pack 1300; item 62 is made from item 61 (Painter Howell) at trigger 0; Seradar removes only items 10 and 11.
- The order of the 1324 puzzle (either order works; stage 2 first avoids the camera pan), the pad touch sequence of 1299 and the exit door.
- The 1300, 1335, 1344, 1324 and 1327 teleport and trigger volumes.
- Positions of the talkers and the target (Howell, Seradar, the Shooting Spellbook) from the character regions named by the entity tables, and the walking routes of goals 102, 104, 114, 117, 119, 135 and 138, built from the pack ground, the climb panels and the stair, ramp and spiral surfaces; whether goals 131 and 132 can be skipped.

Open items and defaults.
- Resolved (goal 104): the 18 floor tiles (positions, short and long crumbles), the barrier lines with their damage boxes and the lanes that avoid them are listed in the goal row; the floor is continuous across the whole run. M for the lanes, L for whether a crumbled pad leaves a hole. Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): follow the walk in the row; keep more than 4 units from tile pads.
- Resolved (goal 117): item 11 lies on a branch that is reached only by climbing the tree's wall panels (three tiers, ground to 1564.2); the grab fires from the ground with the stick pushed into the panel. M for the step from the panels onto the branch. Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): as in the row; report blocked after 90 s.
- Resolved (goals 135 and 138): the walk from the 1324 entry down to the lower floor and up to the bell platform is a descending spiral then a ramp (waypoints in the rows, about 610 units from the entry to the bells). M.
- Resolved (goals 114, 119, 120 and 122): Painter Howell (entity 18), Seradar (entity 43) and the target (entity 0) are not in embedded blocks; the entity tables name character regions 1315, 1333 and 1328, and the positions are in the rows. H for the points, M for the walks.
- Resolved (goal 102): the stairs to the 1098 second floor are two mirror flights with a switchback and a gallery (waypoints in the row, about 200 units). M.
- Resolved (goals 131 and 132): flags 402 and 403 are read by nothing outside those two triggers, so both goals may be skipped (H).

### Act 2 automated walk: inputs and assertions

Purpose: turn goals 100 to 155 into a script a bot can run against the rewrite with no human help: exact inputs, waits, end-of-goal assertions and failure handling. Everything below is derived from the goal rows above, SPEC-0033 (stick, buttons, climbing, blocks), SPEC-0022 (dialogue), SPEC-0052 and SPEC-0026 (movies, fades, movie skip), SPEC-0028 (pads), SPEC-0041 and SPEC-0048 (1324), SPEC-0036 (Roberta's attacks), SPEC-0021 (quick panel) and SPEC-0067 sections 2, 4, 8, 11 and 12. It is answered from the game code and disc data only; the original was not run. Waypoints are not repeated: "the row's list" means the waypoint list in that goal's row above. Confidence tags: H, M, L as in the goal list. A test interface is assumed (a rewrite choice, not a game rule): the bot can read flags, held items and counts, the current pack, the hero's position, facing, state, health, magic and swap mode, the camera's forward vector, whether a dialogue box is open and whether its page is complete, whether hero control is free (not in a scripted scene, box, movie or fade), whether a movie is playing, the pad-puzzle state, block positions, the journal list and a log of "teleported" and "blocked" reports.

**Input primitives (H unless marked).**

1. Ticks and units: 60 ticks per second; distances in world units; run speed 19.29 and walk speed 13.51 units per second (SPEC-0067 3.2).
2. Stick bytes (SPEC-0033, Stick scaling): a stick axis is a byte; 127 is neutral and bytes 88 to 167 are the dead zone. For a scaled axis value a in the range minus 1 to plus 1, where a is positive for "pushed down or right": byte = 167.5 + 87.5 a if a is above zero, 87.5 + 87.5 a if below, 127 if zero, rounded to the nearest whole byte. Forward (push up) is a low Y byte; left is a low X byte. Test vectors: forward 1.0, side 0 gives Y 0, X 127; forward and left at 0.7071 each give Y 26, X 26 (25.6 rounded up); straight back gives Y 255; straight right gives X 255.
3. Steering rule (world-relative goal, camera-relative stick): the goals give world positions, but the hero turns the stick into a direction using the camera's flattened forward vector C and the screen-left vector L (the up vector crossed with C; SPEC-0033, Stick direction). For each tick: take the flat direction w from the hero to the current waypoint, compute f = w dot C (forward component) and l = w dot L (left component), and set the stick pair to (forward f, left l) at full length 1.0 (speed value 1.0). Convert to bytes with rule 2 using a_Y = minus f and a_X = minus l. Recompute every tick because the camera moves (it swings during scenes and slopes). Calibration check at the start of the first walk leg (M for the left sign): the hero's heading after 10 ticks must be within 20 degrees of w; if it is nearly the mirror image about C, flip the sign of l for the rest of the run and report it.
4. Run: hold R1 on every walk leg (running needs R1 held, and starts only if the hero is already running or has not run for 0.5 s; SPEC-0033 5.1). Without R1 a full push walks at 13.51. Release R1 and use stick length 0.5 (walk tier) for the last 4 units of any leg that ends in a trigger sphere, a pickup or a talk spot. There is no braking: releasing the stick moves one more step (about 0.32 at run speed) and then stops.
5. Turning limits: while moving, the facing turns at most 18.19 degrees per tick toward the stick direction, and a stick exactly opposite the facing never turns the hero (SPEC-0033, Facing turn). So when a leg reverses the heading, first aim 10 degrees off the reverse bearing. To turn in place before a shot or a grab, push the stick toward the wanted bearing at length 0.35 for ceil(angle / 18) plus 1 ticks, then release; this moves the hero less than 0.7 (slow-walk tier, about 0.06 per tick).
6. Waypoint following: advance when within 1.5 of an interior waypoint and within 1.0 of the final point; the lists in the rows are wall-checked with a 1.175 hero radius, so corner cutting is not allowed: always aim at the next waypoint, never at a later one.
7. Button taps: a tap holds the button 2 ticks and releases it for at least 6 ticks (press edges are what the game reads). Square (S) is the action button: talk, door and teleport prompts, the bed, grabbing a block, advancing a dialogue box. Circle (C) is the attack (Roberta, once she owns the wand). Cross (X) is the jump. Triangle uses the quick-panel marker. Up and Down open the quick panels. A hold is the same button held for the stated number of ticks without a gap.
8. Stray presses: an S with no prompt, talk or box makes the hero sneak while held, and an S with the weapon drawn and no target puts the weapon away (SPEC-0033, Controls). So the bot sends S only when (a) a prompt or talk candidate is on, (b) a dialogue box is open, or (c) the goal row says so. An attack tap (C) with no weapon does nothing before goal 120.
9. Jump and climb: X is a tap with the stick held toward the wanted heading; a standing jump rises about 3.2. A wall-panel climb needs no button: face the panel and push the stick at full length against its normal (distance to the plane within minus 1.175 to 1.47; SPEC-0033 climbing entry rules); keep the stick pushed until the hero's height stops rising. A ledge grab after a jump is automatic when the ledge lies in the climb-up or hang class (SPEC-0033 Behavior 51): hold the stick toward the ledge through the hang and the pull-up.
10. Blocks (SPEC-0033 4A.2, SPEC-0048): stand within 1.6 to 1.9 of a block face (the grab gap must be under 2.0) with the face lit, tap S when the grab label shows, and the hero walks to the grab spot, which is 1.5 from the face (half the block size plus 1.5 from the block centre). While latched, a stick push toward the block (world direction through the block) pushes, a push away pulls, and a push along the face moves the block sideways; one move starts per clip cycle (Roberta 44 ticks for push and pull, 36 for a side move; the move itself finishes in 18, 9 and 7 ticks). The block moves one step of half its size per move. Keep the stick held between moves and release it (or switch its direction) as soon as the block position test of the goal is reached. The hero rides with the block, so the bot reads the block's position, not the hero's.
11. Healing from the quick panel (SPEC-0021, Quick-item panel): Red Juppie (150) is entry 1 (Up) and Purple Juppie (149) is entry 3 (Down). Red: tap Up (opens the panel with Up selected), tap Cross. Purple: tap Up, tap Down (selects entry 3), tap Cross. Cross sets the marker and uses the item; later uses of the same item can be Triangle with the panel closed. Red heals Roberta 32 health, Purple 40 magic (SPEC-0067 3.8). The kit holds three of each.
12. Movie skip (SPEC-0026): a movie can be skipped by holding Cross or Start for 5 counted frames, but only after at least one pass in which neither button was held (the check arms itself on that pass). Bot rule: release both for 2 ticks after the movie starts, then hold Cross for 10 ticks. Skipping ends only the current movie; each queued movie needs its own skip. The default run plays every movie in full (movies are part of the acceptance test: length checks below); "fast mode" skips them.

**Dialogue handling (SPEC-0022, Reveal and paging; H).** All Act 2 text uses the shared dialogue box. The only key that advances or closes a box is Square (press edge): on a page that is still revealing, the first press shows the page at once and the next press moves to the next page; on the last page the press closes the box (label "Finish"; an empty last page still needs its press). Circle and Triangle answer yes or no prompts, and no Act 2 level module opens a prompt, so they are never needed; Cross held only makes the reveal 5 times faster and is not used. A press in the first update of a page is ignored, so the bot never sends a second S in the tick after a page opens. Bot rule: whenever a box is open, send one S tap every 12 ticks (0.2 s) until no box is open; with the page-complete signal, send S only on a tick when the page is complete (pages needed = presses needed). After the last box closes, wait for control free (the script ends the conversation a few ticks later) before any other input; never send S in the 15 ticks after a box closes. Extra boxes can open by themselves while the bot walks (the Kekou tour lines in pack 1335 before the bed, the reminder line every 60 s in pack 1335 after movie 22 while flag 239 is off, the pack 1324 hint at arrival, the line 0 of pack 1174 after the Sisters break the south wall twice), so the box rule runs on every tick of every goal, not only in talk goals.

Pages and presses per line on the route (page counts and character counts read from the line tables; reveal time is characters divided by 20 per second, so a full unskipped page of 100 characters takes 5 s; the minimum is one press per page after a complete reveal, the maximum two per page when the first press only completes the reveal):

**Movie waits (SPEC-0052 Act 2 movie list, SPEC-0026; H for lengths).** A queued movie plays to its end unless skipped; the next queued movie follows at once. The bot sends no input during a movie except the skip rule, and the timeout of a goal that contains movies is the sum of their lengths plus 40 s. **Fades and waited scene steps the bot must wait out (seconds).** 1174: arrival fade-out then fade-in 0.5 s (goal 103); act-start fade-out 0.3 s (goal 110); 1300: council scene fades 0.7 s out, 0.4 s in and others (inside the 80 s scene budget of goal 113); 1335 bed: fade-out 1.0 s, movie, then 1.0 s wait and 1.0 s fade-in (goal 129); trigger 7: fade-out 0.5 s then movie (goal 144); 1324: stage-2 plate scene about 5 s (fade out 0.5, camera 1.63, wait 1.0, fade out and in), bell plate scene about 5 s (camera 4.6), third bell pan 12.7 s, solved scene about 17.7 s (2.0 s wait, 2.0 s fade-out, movie 23, 0.5 s fade-in, ten chimes 2.5 s). The bot never tries to move during these; it waits for control free.

**Failure detection and recovery (rewrite choice for the test bot; each report goes to a log as BLOCKED, TELEPORTED, DEAD or HUNG).**

1. Per-goal timeout (column "Timeout" below). Walk legs use route length / 19.29 s times 1.5 plus the waits of the goal, and never less than 30 s; blocked is reported after 120 s except the 1335 spiral leg of goal 129 (150 s). On timeout the goal is BLOCKED unless it has an allowed fallback below.
2. Stuck detection on a walk leg: if the hero moves less than 10 units in 3 s while a non-zero stick is sent and control is free (no box, scene, movie or lock), do in order: (a) release the stick 0.5 s and re-aim at the waypoint; (b) tap X once; (c) step 3 units sideways at right angles to the leg and re-aim; (d) back up to the previous waypoint and repeat the leg once; (e) after two full retries report BLOCKED. Stuck detection is off while a block is held, during a climb, a pull-up, a lesson or a scripted scene.
3. Hung scene: if control stays locked more than the scene budget plus 30 s with no box open and no movie playing, tap S once (a hidden box may wait) and wait 10 s more; then report HUNG.
4. Hostile characters (goals 101, 102, 129 to 134, 143 to 145, 146 to 153, 154; SPEC-0067 section 8): the bot never fights. Rule set: keep running; pass hidden minions at the longest distance the route allows; a Warrior or Dark Knight must be left behind by running (they cannot be killed with the starting weapons); Roberta heals with Red Juppie (rule 11) when her health is below 40 percent of her maximum, and with Purple when her magic is below 40 percent only if the next goal needs magic (goals 122 to 127 only); if a minion blocks a one-lane route for more than 3 s, shoot it with C taps every 25 ticks, facing it within 30 degrees, until it falls or 20 s pass (cost 30 to 95 magic; Warriors cannot be killed, so go round them); with the practice lessons done magic is not otherwise needed. Tor in pack 1098 cannot hurt the Dark Knights; he runs, and heals with the first healing item of his quick panel below 40 percent health.
5. Death is game over in all of Act 2 (SPEC-0055); the bot reports DEAD and the test fails. The harness may reload the last snapshot only at the save points of SPEC-0067 3.6 (none in packs 1174, 1344, 1324 and 1299); the walk never saves.
6. Allowed teleport fallbacks (rewrite choice, automated player only; a human player is never teleported; each is reported as "teleported" and the test result is "pass with teleport", not "pass"): goal 100 (load pack 1023 at the entry the exit from 1230 gives, only after 240 s); goal 102 (place at (21.0, -107.6, 57.0) in pack 1098 after 120 s); goal 104 (place at (-10.0, -128.1, -21.0) in pack 1174 after 90 s without the climb, or after 120 s in total); goal 117 (place at (-119.0, 1557.6, -57.0) in pack 1300 after 90 s); goal 133 (place at (-41.5, 2.83, -129.5) in pack 1344 after 120 s, the row's own default). No other goal may be teleported; a block or climb that fails there is a defect of the rewrite and is reported BLOCKED.

**Order of the walk between goals 112 and 118 (rewrite choice, saves several hundred units).** The goal numbers keep their meaning; goals 115 to 117 are free in order (SPEC-0058, "Goals that are free in order or optional"). The bot runs them as 112, 116, 113, 114, 117, 115, 118. Reason: the Stone of Illumination lies on the entry terrace, 197 units from the arrival and joined to the entry route, whereas fetched after the post it would cost about 865 units from the post. Route lengths in this order (units): arrival to the Stone 197, Stone back to (1.5, 1513.7, 72.0) about 180, council from there about 300, council end to Howell about 170, Howell to the tree foot about 120, canopy to the post about 570 (after a harmless drop from the canopy), post to the east door about 190. These distances are sums of the rows' waypoint lists; the joins between lists are not wall-checked (M).

**Journal rows after each goal (SPEC-0049, SPEC-0067 section 10; rows 68 to 89, 165 and 166 only; H for the flags, M for the walk order):** after 101 {68}; 102 {69}; 111 {70}; 113 {70, 72, 165}; 114 adds {166}; 119 adds {73} (rows 80 to 82 never list because the parts are already held); 120 {73 closes, 75 opens}; 122 {76}; 123 {77}; 124 {78}; 125 {79}; 127 {72 and 79 close with 234, 70 closes and 71 opens with 235}; 129 {71 closes}; 130 {83}; 133 {83 closes, 84}; 141 {84 closes, 85}; 143 {85 closes, 86}; 144 {86 closes, 87}; 145 {87 closes, 88}; 153 {88 closes, 89}; 155 {89, 165 and 166 close}. Between steps the list is unchanged (rows 165 and 166 stay from goals 113 and 114 until the Act 3 start, unless the optional side scenes set 397 and 400 earlier).

**Hero health and magic ranges (assertions; M is Roberta's maximum health 120 + 20a, magic maximum 150 + 25b with a, b the Act 1 stat-up counts).** Goals 101 to 109 (Tor): health at least 50 percent of his maximum after 102 and at least 40 percent after 109 (barriers cost 3 per hit; Dark Knights 10 to 50 per hit). Goal 111 to 121: health equals maximum (0 damage sources; regeneration refills to the maximum) and magic equals maximum. Goals 122 and 123: health at least 60 percent (one 30-damage missile may land in lesson 2). Goals 124 to 127: health equals maximum at each lesson end (it is refilled every tick); at goal 127 magic between maximum minus 100 and maximum minus 25 (lesson shots spend 25 to 75; a miss is free) and it does not refill. Goals 128 to 155: health at least 40 percent after every goal, magic never below the value at goal 127 (the bot does not shoot) except when Purple Juppies were used; Juppie counts never rise above 3 each. Wallet equals 1,150 from goal 111 to goal 154 (nothing is bought or picked up that pays). Goal 155: both heroes at full health and magic.

S, C, X and R1 are as above; "box" means the dialogue rule.

**Total expected run time.** Walking about 10,300 units (8 min 55 s at run speed; 10 min allowing for turns and creeps); movies 573.9 s (9 min 34 s); scenes, fades, loads and waited script steps about 5 min 30 s (the council scene 60 s, the lessons' fixed waits 25 s, the bed and 1324 scenes 60 s, the 1174 scenes 60 s, 12 loads at 5 s, the first-arrival scene 15 s); dialogue about 1 min 30 s with the press rule (4 min if every page were read in full); the Sisters' ball wait of goal 104 (0 to 90 s, 30 s typical); the lessons' shooting 40 s; the block, bell and pad puzzles 2 min 30 s. So the default walk with every movie played takes about 30 minutes (range 25 to 38); fast mode with all movies skipped takes about 21 minutes (a skipped movie still costs about 1 s). The sum of the goal timeouts above is the bound for a failing run (about 85 minutes).

**Confidence and limits.** High: stick, button and dialogue rules, page counts, movie lengths, assertions of flags, items, packs and arrival positions (all from the rows and the data above). Medium: the world-to-stick conversion sign (the left vector's side is Medium-High in SPEC-0033), the joins between route lists in the changed walk order, the standing-point facing rules for lessons, the pit climbs of goals 104 and 133, the tree climb exit of goal 117, the helix pad steps (distances of the step-backs), run times of goals 100 and 112 (not in the rows). Not settled: the length of route 100; whether the first-arrival scene of goal 112 returns control before entity 10 reaches locator 41; the exact tick on which control returns after the 1324 plate scenes (the bot polls control free); whether movie 22 leaves pack 1344 or pack 1335 as the current world (the bot asserts the hero's position, not the pack, until settled).

## Goal list for an automated player (Act 3)

Purpose: the ordered goals for walkthrough steps 28 to 43, from the arrival in Brightvale (return world 64 after movie 30) to the end of Act 3 (movie 54 and the return to pack 1300; step 44 and Act 4 are not in this list). Same table form, kinds, positions and confidence tags as the Act 1 list (read its "How to read the table" first). Goals are numbered from 300 so that they do not clash with Act 1 (1 to 99) and Act 2 (100 on). Confidence is per row (H High, M Medium, L Low); a row marked M or L names the link that is not settled.

Act 3 conventions.
- Heroes. Act 3 starts with swap mode 2 (free) and Tor selected (SPEC-0053, section 3.7). Switch with the L1 and R1 chord. "Hero" in a Needs column names the hero that must be active; "either" means no restriction. The only forced lock in this range is pack 1244 (step 38), whose last scene sets mode 0 (Tor only) until the Werelupe King is dead; its death scene sets mode 2 again (goals 386 to 391).
- Walking legs. As in Act 1, a leg between packs needs no goal of its own beyond the aim point given. The ground neighbour chains used here (pack: neighbours) are: 886: 704, 699, 717; 704: 886, 880; 880: 704, 699, 702; 702: 880, 753, 1238; 1238: 702, 713; 713: 1238, 722, 735; 722: 713 (its five door interiors hang off it); 735: 713; 699: 886, 1230; 991: 699, 705; 705: 991, 986, 989; 696: 1023, 1056, 1230, 893, 1184, 1066; 893: 696, 851; 851: 893, 822, 842, 800; 822: 851, 861; 861: 822, 864; 864: 861, 874; 1250: 1255, 1256, 1254, 1251, 717; 1256: 1250, 1257; 1255: 1250, 1244, 1245, 1246; 1129: 1130; 1130: 1129, 1080; 1080: 1130, 685, 1079; 685: 1080, 1078; 1079: 1080, 1081; 1096 and 1081 are joined by a script load, not by cells. The crusher room 1089 and the hazard rooms 1090 and 1091 are side rooms of the Knights Crypts and are not on the main route (goal 452). High for the chains (they come from the pack neighbour lists).
- Script teleports. A "teleport" door is a rectangle of the pack (centre given, width along the long axis); stand inside it and press Square at the prompt (context 13) unless the row says it acts on contact. Pack 880 teleport 0, pack 991 teleports 4, 5 and 7, pack 753 teleport 0, pack 758 teleports 0 and 1, pack 777 teleport 0, pack 722 teleports 0 to 4, pack 1257 teleports 0 to 2, pack 1173 teleport 0 and pack 1152 teleports 0 and 1 are of this kind (SPEC-0006).
- Hero index in level scripts: hero 0 is Tor and hero 1 is Roberta.
- Boss arrivals and exits are given in SPEC-0062 (arrival records); the exit of every boss row below is the scripted one named in the row.

Resolved (volumes): script trigger index 0 has exactly two volumes and either one runs the prompt: the top one above (y 310.0) and a lower one, a rectangle centre (1048.5, 217.3, 247.7), 28.8 wide, 11.3 tall, next to locators 13, 14 and 17 (about (1060.5, 216.8, 264.6)), which is the keep's middle level and the nearer one. The other index-0 records of the pack are solid geometry, not prompts. The keep is a vertical climb: from the arena floor (y 102 to 126) ladders lead up to a west ledge (ladder tops at y 144.5 and 146, locators 4 to 7 at y 123 to 155), then climb panels at (951.8, 157.8, 236.7) and (971.3, 180.2, 236.9) lead to locator 8 (977.7, 185.5, 246.7), a tall panel run reaches locator 9 (977.7, 261.8, 246.7), and panels at (1062.7, 241.9, 208.6) and (1037.6, 257.1, 188.7) lead to the top. Resolved (the climb above y 145, with walls; M-L as a whole, the parts are marked). (a) The ladder tops at y 144.5 and 146 near (899.9, 146.0, -21.5) are a trap, not the way: the platform there (y 143.4, x 901 to 919, z -21 to -4) is a breakable floor, and script triggers 2 and 3 (rectangles at (908.8, 142.4, -17.8), 7.0 wide, and (906.7, 140.0, -23.3), 16.2 wide) break it and throw the hero by a long fall to locator 11 (911.8, 104.4, -11.2). Stay off them and use the south-west ladder. H. (b) Arena leg (x, y, z): (1094.7, 101.7, -1.5) arrival; (1002.0, 101.7, 86.0); (933.5, 101.7, 86.0), through the gap in the dividing wall at z 76 to 104 (x 946 to 962); then to the foot of ladder g14, (888.5, 101.7, 131.0), and up it (34.6 tall, top (885.6, 136.1, 132.0)). The wall records place a solid slab at z 112 to 117 (x 862 to 921, top 125 to 130) between the gap and the foot, and the wall-aware search left the last 20 units open, so this leg is L; the ground-only model reaches the foot directly. (c) West ledge (M): (884.7, 135.2, 131.0); (882.2, 141.7, 139.7); (883.5, 137.1, 174.7); (892.2, 139.9, 196.0); (887.2, 142.4, 218.5); (907.2, 147.6, 238.5); (949.7, 142.0, 239.7); climb panel up at (951.8, 157.8, 236.7) to (953.5, 157.7, 241.0); (969.7, 157.7, 241.0); climb panel up at (971.3, 180.2, 236.9) to (973.5, 180.0, 239.7); locator 8 (977.7, 185.5, 246.7). (d) To the middle level (M): (1007.2, 180.1, 251.0); (1028.5, 191.4, 226.0); (1036.0, 197.6, 233.5); (1024.7, 204.8, 233.5) (ledge climbs of 3 to 4); (1036.0, 207.8, 219.7), the plateau at y 207.8; (1058.5, 207.8, 237.2). The platform of locators 13, 14 and 17 (top 216.3 to 221.4; edge records from (1079.1, 239.5) to (1061.2, 261.2) and from (1059.0, 261.0) to (1056.8, 263.6), their outward normals facing south-west) is 8.8 above the plateau, so no jump reaches it. The pack's one movable block is the stair: it starts at (1050.82, 207.78, 258.82), size 3.98, height 2.83, step 1.99 per push, first axis +z and second axis +x, pushes available +z 2, +x 3, -z 4, -x 3. Push it 3 times toward +x (stand west of it; it ends at x 1056.80, top 210.6). Then stand on it, face north-east toward the edge near (1058.9, 261.0) and jump (Cross): the jump lifts the feet to 213.8, the edge top is 216.05 after the 0.25 allowance, 2.25 above the feet and inside the hang limit of 3.5, so the hero grabs the edge, hangs and pulls up onto (1061.0, 216.4, 262.2) (SPEC-0033 Behaviors 51 and 51A; the ground probe ignores the crate, so the grab is a hang, not a mantle). Locator 13 (1060.5, 216.8, 264.6) is 3 away; walk north-west across the platform to the lower volume at (1048.5, 217.3, 247.7), 28.8 wide, and press Square. (e) The top volume (optional, L): from (1061.0, 216.4, 262.2): (1088.5, 234.3, 224.7); panel at (1062.7, 241.9, 208.6) to (1057.2, 241.1, 203.5); panel at (1037.6, 257.1, 188.7) to (1038.5, 257.1, 184.7); (1022.2, 257.1, 182.2); (979.7, 257.1, 234.7), by locator 9 (977.7, 261.8, 246.7); then the ring up to (1022.2, 310.1, 203.5). The lower volume is enough. Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): do legs (b) to (d), push the crate three times toward +x, jump from it to the platform and press Square in the lower volume; report blocked after 240 s. |

Resolved (crates): the pack's three movable blocks are the plate weights. Block 0 starts at (-526.83, 0.93, -433.20): plate 6 is 12.5 north of it and plate 7 is 12.9 west and 12.5 north. Block 1 starts at (-276.49, 0.93, -644.14) beside plate 5, and block 2 at (-397.27, 1.03, -668.08) between plates 3 and 4. All three have size 2.0518, height 8.6086 and a step of 1.0259 per push; the first axis A is +z and the second axis B is +x. The pushes available (+A, +B, -A, -B) are 14, 5, 8, 31 for block 0; 14, 32, 10, 7 for block 1; 11, 26, 20, 21 for block 2. A block fires a plate volume while its position lies inside the plate rectangle widened by 0.6 times its size (1.23) and keeps firing while it rests there (SPEC-0048, Trigger volumes). Plan for plate 7 (rectangle corner (-536.2, -417.2), 7.0 along -x and 7.0 along -z, so widened x -544.4 to -535.0, z -425.4 to -416.0): stand east of block 0 and push it west 10 times (it ends at x -537.09, outside plate 6's widened x range of -531.9 to -522.5), then stand south of it and push it north 10 times (it ends at z -422.94). The minimum is 8 west + 8 north (16 moves); pushing north first fires plate 6, so push west before north. The record opens within about 1.2 s of the first fire, plays its camera once (1.0 s plus 1.5 s) and stays open while the crate rests there, so do not push the crate off. Blocks 1 and 2 are for records 2, 4 and 5 and are not needed. H for the block data; H for the plate rectangle axes (both extents 7.0); L for whether a resting block passes the plate script's shot and attack tests (a block fire carries no actor). Resolved (the climb from the arena to the tower foot): the foot (y -1556.8) is 45 or more above every arena surface (the highest arena ground is y -1597.8 at (509, -615)); no ladder, climb panel, ledge (limit 6.7), block or lift links them, and the pack's ladders and lifts are inside the tower. The way is the Rope Gun (item 36, granted at the Farm; SPEC-0033 Behavior 51, state 27). The pack has two rope aim targets: target 1 at (404.62, -1542.58, -583.94), 14 above the foot, switched on by the tower door scene (level state 16, flag 317), and target 0 at (449.93, -1324.46, -624.42), high in the tower. Procedure on the arena floor: stand idle (state 1), push the right stick up or down to start the aim mode, turn the camera until target 1 passes the range test (camera eye to target strictly below 110) and the facing test (camera forward dot unit direction above 0.998), then hold R2 (Tor; for Roberta her R2 choice). The sight line from the eye lowered 1.5 is tested every 5 units against frame records and class-3 volumes, and the rim of the foot slab (outline top y -1556.8, 5.8 deep) blocks it from almost the whole floor: for camera eyes 4 to 8 above the feet and 8 to 20 behind, only spots within about 7 units of the point below the target stay clear, (406, -1614.6, -582) and (408, -1614.6, -578). The rope then reels the hero 0.8 per tick toward the rope target until the rope end is within 3.0 of the weapon point; the hero lets go and lands on the foot near (404, -1556.8, -584). Foot waypoints (x, y, z): (404.1, -1556.8, -575.3); (392.8, -1556.8, -577.8), the case trigger 0 cylinder at (393.4, -1556.8, -578.0), on the lift platform (x 383 to 404, z -589 to -568, whose lift is driven by the prompts). Gaps settled in this list.
- Heroes: the Werelupe King fight is locked to Tor by the swap mode 0 that pack 1244's last scene sets (mode 2 returns at the King's death); Juppie in Act 3 is a Roberta fight (the dazed loop reads only her shots); the Faerie Sisters shield needs one hero shot (Roberta); the Plague Serpent, Black Knight, Undead Draik, Gelert and Ixi fights accept either hero.
- Puzzle orders: library 5, 6, 7, 8 with the entry scene first (777); the water-channel levers 7, 6, 7, 5 and the test button (1130); the three levers of 1249 in any order; the four crusher buttons within 18.4 s; the five Oubliette lever flags and the plates (1078); the three-level tower prompts (1096).
- Item sources: berries 5, 4 and 3 in the swamp (735); item 23 only from Guildmaster Marl; item 126 from pack 842 trigger 2; item 29 dropped by the Death Knight AI 10 of 1247 (starts at (-135.1, 19.4, -139.7)); item 18 dropped by the fifth Restless Spirit of 685 (starts at (-349.1, -12.2, -706.3), in the channel past the Oubliette ramp); item 103 given by the scene of 1081; item 117 in 1096.
- Positions settled: the witch Sophie at (-22.3, 0.0, 5.6) in 1251 (goal 370); Otto (-1044.8, 46.6, 1826.7) and Elspeth (-1085.6, 33.4, 1823.9) in 874 (goal 359; entity 6 is Otto, entity 7 is Elspeth); Rory the Shady Guy, start (392.4, 27.5, 697.7) in 1023 (goal 405); the Brightvale road guards on the road to teleport 0 of 880, loaded by the road rectangle or sphere (goal 336); the 1079 gate walls inside their trigger volumes (goal 429); the cell exits 1098 to 1121 to 1122 (goals 443 and 444); the Oubliette entrance as a gate trap with the key found inside (goals 421 to 424); the Rope Gun as the only way up the glade shaft (goal 397).
- Walking chains for every leg (above) from the pack neighbour lists.

Open items and defaults.
- Resolved: the entity positions of the interior talkers (witch in 1251, miners in 874, cloak seller in 1023) are in the rows of goals 370, 359 and 405. Pack 685 has no breakables and the 12 breakables of 1081 drop only empty placements, so no breakable matters in either pack; the items come from characters (goals 421 and 432).

Resolved: the guard group trigger of 880 (goal 336), the item-29 source (goal 375), the item-18 source (goal 421), the cell exits of 1098, 1121 and 1122 (goals 443, 444, 448) and the rope route of 956 (goal 397: the rope is required, order 2, 1, 0).
- Resolved (Medium; goals 420, 421, 425, 426): the Oubliette needs the crate puzzle, not a held plate. The corridor gaps of 685 are bridged by thin solid slabs on both rails (one hop with a running jump; no jump alone crosses the 20.2 wide gap, whose reach is 14.0); at the first visit all six door records are closed and the platform side reaches only the first rooms; push crate 0 west and north onto plate 7 (record 0), then take the route of goal 421 to the channel, pull lever 26 (mandatory, it opens door B for the way back) and return by the route of goal 425. The second hero cannot hold a plate (the parked hero is not in the world). Remaining doubts: the plate rectangle axes, whether a resting crate passes the plate script's tests, and the standing behaviour on the 0.35 thick slabs. The gate-trap reading of goals 422 to 424 (object poses) is Medium.
- Resolved in part (Medium-Low; goals 342 and 439): the 1096 climb from the arena to the tower foot is the Rope Gun at aim target 1 (404.62, -1542.58, -583.94) from the arena floor just below it (goal 439); the keep climb of 986 above y 145 is a ladder, panel and ledge chain with the pack's crate used as a stair (goal 342), whose arena leg (ladder foot at (888.5, 101.7, 131.0)) and top leg are not closed against the walls. The order of the 1096 tower case triggers is fixed by the arming words and the heights; the join of the tower foot's lift platform to the prompt ledge and the Rope Gun chain of goal 397 stay Medium-Low.
- Whether trigger 12 of pack 956 (goal 398) or the fight is reached first depends on the order the heroes walk; the rows give the fight first.

## Goal list for an automated player (Act 4)

Purpose: the ordered goals for walkthrough steps 44 to 46, from the return to pack 1300 after movie 54 (the end of the Act 3 list, goal 450) through the rescue of Queen Fyora in pack 1428, the Act 4 start, the four Altador shrines, the waking of Jerdana in pack 1382, the Darkest Faerie fight in pack 1432, the finale, the epilogue in pack 1300 and the credits. Same table form, kinds, positions and confidence tags as the Act 1 list (read its "How to read the table" first). Goals are numbered from 500, so they do not clash with Act 1 (1 to 99), Act 2 (100 to 155) or Act 3 (300 to 452). Confidence is per row (H High, M Medium, L Low); a row marked M or L names the link that is not settled.

Act 4 conventions.
- Row fields. Every row's notes open with three short fields. "H:" names the hero that must be active ("either" means no restriction; Tor is the default pick when a hero is needed for melee and Roberta when one is needed for shots). "SW:" names every hero switch in the row, by the player (L1 and R1 chord) or by the script ("none" when there is none). "AR:" names the arena entry and exit when the row starts, runs in or leaves a closed space (a boss room, a shrine pack or a trial room), and reads "none" otherwise.
- Added kinds. SWITCH: press the L1 and R1 chord to change the active hero; it works only while the swap mode allows it (SPEC-0053, section 3.1). SHOOT: fire the active hero's quick shots (Roberta) at a named target; each hit that counts pays the magic cost (SPEC-0036, Magic). STRIKE: stand inside a named cylinder and make a melee attack (Square) while the hero's body overlaps the named box.
- Rectangle triggers are given by reference corner (x, y, z), the two horizontal or in-plane extents, and the direction of the first extent. Sphere and cylinder triggers are given by centre or base and radius. Teleport rectangles ("teleport" below) are entered by standing inside and pressing Square at the context 13 prompt, except where a row says contact is enough.
- Heroes. The Act 4 start (goal 514) leaves swap mode 2 (free) with the selected hero unchanged and heals both heroes (SPEC-0053, section 3.7), so the player may switch freely except where a row names a scripted lock: pack 1428 locks to Roberta (mode 1) only if she goes down before the shot meter completes, and ends the game if Tor is selected at that moment (goal 503); pack 1394 sets mode 3 and switches the hero by script at each lever (goals 543 to 546); pack 1432 sets mode 3 at the fight entry and swaps by script (goals 577 to 583). Hero 0 is Tor and hero 1 is Roberta in the scripts.
- Walking chains (pack: ground neighbours). 1300: none on foot (pack 1300 is on the no-swap list, so its cells that name 1344 only stream it in; it is left by script doors only: teleport 2 to 1327, teleport 1 to 1335 while flag 92 is clear, teleport 0 to the interior 1433). 1327 and 1299 are also on the no-swap list (script doors only; 1327 door 1 to 1299, 1299 bottom teleport to 1335 when flag 248 is on). 1335: 1344. 1344: 1335, 1428. 1428: 1344. 1411: 1391 (cells); 1411 to 1363 is a script load (goal 520). 1363: 1370. 1370: 1353, 1350, 1358, 1363. 1391: 1411, 1381. 1381: 1391. 1353 to 1392 is a script load (goal 539). 1392: 1395, 1353. 1395: 1392, 1393. 1393: 1395, 1396. 1396: 1393, 1394. 1350 to 1371, 1358 to 1397, 1363 to 1398 and 1391 to 1382 are script loads. 1432 is entered only by the return world that movie 72 sets. High for the neighbour lists; the exact crossing inside each pair is Medium.
- Items that matter. Item 117 is taken and item 116 is given by the pack 1428 entry scene. Items 25, 26 and 27 (mosaic pieces) are world pickups in pack 1411 (goals 516 to 518). Elemental Motes 160 to 171 are world pickups in the Altador districts (goal 562) and may already be held. Item 172 (Nova Mote) answers every orb colour and is dropped by the final fight's Minion Warriors. Item 50 (Potion of Revival) is optional.

Resolved: region 1380 holds six Minions on the ring (three Brutes and three Wizards) and four more Minion Grunt records that start unplaced and exist only as Grunts a Wizard lobs; a lobbed Grunt that is alive counts until it is dead, and unsummoned ones do not. The wave ends when every placed Minion and every lobbed Grunt is dead. Resolved: of the three Wizards only one lobs Grunts. Their spawn records carry variants 2, 5 and 0 (monster rows 213, 216 and 211); only row 211, the fire variant (the Wizard on the ring point (-0.1, 7.2, -26.5), north of the centre), has the lobbed-summon slot: while the hero is 10 to 60 units away and in front of it and fewer than four of its Grunts live, it lobs one Minion Grunt (a reserved record of the region, landing where the shot comes down), so up to four Grunts exist at once and a lob is repeated after each death. The other two Wizards fire homing or straight shots and never summon. Wave 2's Wizards are variants 1 and 2 (rows 212, 213) and never summon. Kill the fire Wizard first to stop the Grunts. H for the kinds and the one summoner; M for the live-count and lob timing. |

| 530 | WALK | 1350 to 1358 | Back to the hub 1370 and into district 1358 (the Money district). Its teleport 2 is a vertical door plane at x -529.6 (the hero comes from the +x side), spanning z 1.6 down to -6.8 and height 29.1 to 33.5 (corner (-529.6, 29.1, 1.6), extents 8.4 along -z by 4.4 up). It stands on a raised slab (floor height 29.6, x -532 to -519, z -20 to 19). The door for the ground floor at the same x and z is teleport 5 (height 7.7); it works only once flag 695 is set (the Treasury's own second exit sets it) and is not the story door. Resolved route from the hub arrival to the ground floor under the door (654 units, no step above 2.9): west to (-92, 68), down the spiral stair through (-108, 52) height 27.7, (-108, 16) height 18.1, (-128, 4) height 15 and (-132, 52) height 4.8 to (-144, 60) height 0 (the hand-over from pack 1370 to pack 1358), then (-324, 60), (-384, 0) and (-528, 0) at floor height 6.9. The slab is not joined to that floor in the ground samples: beside it stand a narrow ramp strip (x -513 to -510, heights 23.5 to 36.6) and a terrace (heights 20 to 23, x -492 to -381), and none of the three pieces is joined to another by steps of up to 4.5. Re-checked with walls (patch rims and frame records, hero radius 1.175 and height 3.5), the ground probe's 2.5 rise limit and the ledge gates (a climb from a jump needs the top 1.75 to 4.25 above the ground in front, a hang up to 6.7, never on edge types 3 and 6): the slab and an arched strip beside it (heights 23.4 at z 0 rising to 36.6 at z -28 and z 28, x -515 to -509) form one island; the terrace and the roads at height 21 to 25 (x -491 to -221, z -209 to 85) end at x -492 where they ramp down to 20.4, so the road end looks 17.0 from the arched strip at z 0. Correction (found by re-running the ground test with the outline rule that an entry with no outline records always passes): a narrow bridge piece (x -503 to -494, z -30 to 29, height 21.9 to 22.3) stands in that gap, leaving hops of 2 units (road end to piece) and 6 units (piece to strip), both within a running jump, so the roads, the piece, the strip and the slab form one elevated network. No piece of this network joins the hub stair or the ground (the nearest ground of the hub stair at the same height is about 185 units from the roads' ends at (-252, -171) and (-303, 87)), and no object or script of this pack changes any of it. The slab's own exit arrival is record 5 (-527.2, 32.4, -0.7), the upper pair of the Treasury's exit 0 (message 40); the ground-floor door is the lower pair (message 60, record 77). Not settled: how the first visit reaches the upper door. | 534 | WALK | 1397 | Trigger 12: box corner (377.9, -50.7, -325.0), extents 10.0 along x by 17.7 (height 10.2). Walk in: movie 63 plays, 102 and 349 are set and twelve item 139 pickups appear at records 24 to 35. | 700 on | 102 on | H: either. SW: none. AR: inside. The scripts do not describe the route; the floor shows it in part. The room has four tiers: the start room (heights 0 to 22, x 0 to 152; the arrival plate at height 17.3 hangs above its floor, whose record 1 is (2.9, 1.2, 0.0)), the button and statue hall (x 72 to 628, z -144 to 152, heights -9 to 33), the lower halls (x 340 to 492, z -268 to 24, heights -50 to -22) and the box platform (x 340 to 436, z -344 to -300, height -50). The tiers are not joined by steps of up to 3.2. The objects raised by the buttons (9, 10 and 11) carry no floor or wall data, so they do not join the start room to the hall. The start room (plate and floor together) reaches x 154 and no further in every state of the lifts, the seesaw objects 0 to 3 and the grate, with walks, falls, ledge climbs of up to 12 and running jumps of up to 14 all allowed. Join start room to hall (NOT settled, Low): the module has no hero move, door or teleport between the two (its hero moves are the two arrival spots, the button, lever and statue spots and the trigger 12 spot; its two door callbacks are the west doors that leave for pack 1358), no rope-gun target exists in the pack, and no wall gate bit is ever set. The start room is closed on the hall side by one-sided tall walls that belong to the pack's own collision: a wall across x 152.5 (z -40.5 to 40.5, heights 14.0 to 46.9) in front of an 11.5-wide void (x 154.8 to 166.3) before the hall's edge at height 16.9, and two round bays (x 98 to 113, z +-41 to +-50) at the same heights. They face into the start room, so a hero coming from the hall is not stopped by them. There are no hall corridors at z +-46 that continue the ring walkway: the hall's floor patch answers a ground search at exactly z 46.0 and -46.0 along the whole ring side (a zero-width effect of its outline, found only when a search lattice hits that exact z) and nowhere beside those lines, so an earlier route through (122, 46) and (160, 46) does not exist. The real gap is the void: the ring (height 16.0) ends at x about 154.2, the pit floor (height 0, with a drop edge facing east) at x 154.5 to 154.7, and the hall's floor starts at x 166.3 (height 16.88) for every z from -64 to 64 (a gap of about 12 at ring height). A running jump (up to 14) crosses it, but only from the ring's east strip (x 152.5 to 154.2, z -40 to 42), which the one-sided walls at x 152.5 shut off from the start room; the pit floor is 16.9 below the hall's edge, too low to jump up. The one other ground touching both areas is a raised gantry (height 32.9, x 72 to 99, z 25 to 97) that ends above the start room's central pillar and ramps down into the hall near z 93 to 135; it is 14.5 above the highest ground the start room reaches and the pillar below it (a type 3 wall to 18.6, crown at 26.5) cannot be climbed. Why static data cannot settle it: nothing in the module changes those walls, yet the game must let the hero out, so either a hero-collision rule that lets the hero through the walls is not modelled or a jump route past the pillar needs a physical test. From the hall's east end the route to the box is resolved: about 525 units (west along z -36 at height -7 from (588, -36) to (508, -34), one fall of 17.4 near (490, -20) to height -24.5, then (440, -10) at -34.8, (400, -14) at -36.1, down the ramp to (400, -54) at -44.9 and (382, -90) at -50.1, north along x 378 to 396 to (396, -284), (396, -324) and the box). The two dips (and a third near z -164) of the earlier route are not climbs of 12: each dip holds a lift platform (objects 29, 30 and 31, 15 wide, spanning the whole dip, z -180 to -148, -240 to -208 and -301 to -269) that stands at about -60.6 and rises 10.48 to about -50.1, level with the hall (-50.1), when its block plate is fired (Resolved, High: the plate scene plays the lift animation; see below). The block plates are script triggers 11, 10 and 9 (boxes 3.1 tall with horizontal sides of 1.0 to 1.6 at (366.3, -51.6, -106.1), (406.6, -51.6, -197.8) and (366.6, -51.6, -258.4)), which fire when a movable block comes within 2.954 of the footprint (block starts and push counts in SPEC-0048). Resolved (High): what raises the lifts. Each plate runs a lift scene, but only for one block: plate 11 for block 3, plate 10 for block 1, plate 9 for block 2, once each and one scene at a time; a hero touching a plate, or another block, does nothing and uses nothing up. The scene takes control of the hero and the three guards, plays camera track 2, 3 or 4, starts two effects, waits 1 s and then 2 s, plays the lift animation at 0.2 speed (lift 29 for plate 11, lift 30 for plate 10, lift 31 for plate 9: about 6.3 s for lift 29 and 6.7 s for the others), keeps the hero for 7 s more, stops the effects, and for plates 11 and 10 swaps the invisible block-floor object (plate 11 shows object 33; plate 10 hides it and shows object 34) and re-places the three blocks on the floor then drawn; it ends about 1 s after the camera returns. With flag 102 already set at load the lifts start raised and the plates are not registered. Resolved (Medium): plate 9 can be reached. The plates must be used in the order 11, 10, 9, because at load the blocks stand on the pack's own floor entry, which ends at z -149.5, before the first dip; object 33's floor (outline to z -210.1) lets block 1 cross the first dip, and object 34's floor (outline to z -270.8) lets block 2 cross the second. Plate 11: block 3 in 3 pushes toward -z. Plate 10: block 1 in 47 moves (4 west, 32 south, 11 east) once plate 11 has fired. Plate 9: block 2 in 82 moves (12 west, 45 south, 1 east, 15 south, 4 west, 5 south, ending near (370.0, -256.4)) once plate 10 has fired. The floor objects cover the lift columns at the hall level (x 363 to 378, 379.5 to 395 and 394 to 409), so after plate 11 the hero can cross the first dip, and after plate 10 the second; the walk stops in the third dip (z -301 to -269) until plate 9 raises lift 31. With the lifts down and no plate fired the walk stops at z -148, at the first dip, and a hero cannot climb out of a dip (a hang reaches 6.7). Joins considered and ruled out by the data (Low overall; no change to the join): a world swap (pack 1397 is on the no-swap list, so no neighbour pack can supply ground), an object (the objects 0 to 3, 6 and 7 of the start room, the grate 8 and the button objects 9 to 11 either carry floor data that stays inside the start room or none), a script move, door, teleport or region (the module has none between the two), a rope target (none in the pack) and a wall gate (no mask is ever set), and a patch dropped by the ground test (the pack has no patch with an empty outline list). The only things between the two are the pack's own one-sided walls, whose lower edge is 14.0 above the pit floor, and the void. Default if unresolved (rewrite choice, for the start-room join only): let the hero pass the vault walls (the two wall groups described above), walk the ring to its east end at (154.0, 16.0, 38.0), run and jump east to the hall's west edge at (167.0, 16.88, 38.0) (12.8 on the level, within the jump); if the hero is still in the start room 120 s after arriving (the hall's buttons and statues of goals 532 and 533 come after the join), place him on the hall edge at (170.0, 16.88, 38.0); then path-find to (382.9, -50.7, -316.2) after goal 533 with falls allowed; report blocked after 120 s. H for the plate scenes and which block and lift go with each plate, M for the block move counts (the hero's walk to each grab spot is tested at the spot only), M for the route from the hall east end (given the lifts), L for the start-room join |

The movable blocks (block 0 at (-90.75, -2.83, -1220.52), blocks 1 and 2 at (-229.70, -3.90, -1342.74) and (-229.70, -3.90, -1348.19); SPEC-0048) stay on their own floor patches and cannot bridge a gap, so they are not part of any leg.
